## Snapshot
Coherent is a vertically-integrated photonics maker that has re-rated into one of the cleanest large-cap plays on AI optical interconnect. The story is the Data Center & Communications segment, now ~75% of revenue (Q3 FY26, 2026-05-06), spanning datacom transceivers (800G/1.6T), DCI/scale-across coherent transceivers (ZR/ZR+), optical circuit switching (OCS), and an emerging co-packaged optics (CPO) franchise. The legacy Industrial segment (lasers, engineered materials, semicap) is the smaller, cyclical balance. Coherent's structural edge is owning the indium phosphide (InP) supply chain — EMLs, CW lasers, photodiodes — which is the binding constraint for the entire transceiver industry. Per the 10-K (FY25, 2025-08-15) the legacy reporting structure is three segments (Networking, Materials, Lasers); management now guides on the DC&Comms vs Industrial split. CEO Jim Anderson (ex-Lattice), CFO Sherri Luther.

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) Datacom transceivers (800G/1.6T) · 2) DCI/scale-across coherent transceivers (ZR/ZR+) · 3) InP components (EMLs, CW lasers, photodiodes) + emerging OCS/CPO |
| **Main buyer(s)** | Hyperscalers (concentrated demand) + system OEMs; NVIDIA a lead CPO customer (multi-year supply deal + $2B equity stake) |
| **Key suppliers** | InP substrates (China-sourced, ~70%+ of global output) · merchant EMLs from Lumentum (~50% AI EML share) · rare-earth minerals & noble gases |

Neither is a revenue datapoint.**
- 🔴 **WHY THE RATING SPLIT IS THE ACTUAL SIGNAL HERE, AND IT IS UNCOMFORTABLE FOR THIS PAGE'S "SECOND SOURCE IS ENOUGH" FRAMING: this wiki tracks COHR and LITE as the two-name UHP-laser/OCS duopoly, and the standing framing (from the BTG expert call 06-25 and PhotonCap 07-29) is that only COHR and LITE are a reliable source. The note's COHR content is two product lines in a beneficiary list. It is a relative-preference mark, and that is all it is.**
- ⚠️ **NOT NEW TO THE WIKI, AND THE PROVENANCE MATTERS: the 6D-torus thesis was already logged on [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md) and [[GOOG]] on 08-18 from a SemiAnalysis LinkedIn post, alongside a Virgo scale-out excerpt marked "SOURCE UNCONFIRMED." Today's ingest resolves that excerpt to FUNDA, which means the 08-18 "two sources" were partly one. GF Securities is the genuinely independent second vote — and the first with a rating.** Also hold the unit distinction: **SemiAnalysis's "ICI links 6 → 12" is 2x on LINKS (copper + optical); FUNDA/GF's "1.5 → 6" is 4x on wrap-around OPTICAL PORTS. Not competing estimates — different quantities.**
- **The architecture, in one pass, because it is the same derivation logged in full on [[GOOG]] and [[LITE]]:** the 64-chip rack is reorganised **4×4×4 → 2⁶**, ring edge length falls 4→2, every chip becomes a boundary node in every dimension, and wrap-around optical ports per chip go **1.5 → 6** (**4x**; GF: external optical links per block **96 → 384**, aggregate TPU-to-port **1:8.5** with scale-out at ~1:2.5). Worst-case diameter on 4,096 chips halves (**~24 → ~12 hops**), compressing MoE all-to-all tail latency.
- **THE LEG THAT MATTERS MOST TO COHR'S MIX, AND IT IS DATED: COHERENT LITE.** FUNDA argues that once OCS ratios rise, **insertion loss (Palomar ~2 dB; Lumentum R300 1.5-3 dB) consumes IMDD's ~4 dB FR4 link budget**, so **Coherent Lite adoption starts with 2.4T systems in 2027 and fully transitions at 3.2T (400G/lane) in 2028**, with longer reach further favoured as scale-up superpods grow past 9,600 chips toward 10,000-20,000+. Assumed ASPs: **~$700 for 1.6T DR8 (IMDD) vs ~$1,500 for 2.4T Coherent Lite** — a ~2.1x ASP step. GF's recommendation list is likewise **"OCS supply chain: Lumentum (Buy) & Coherent (Hold)"** plus an unnamed **"2.4T coherent lite transceiver supplier."** ➤ **For COHR specifically the read is mix-favourable and timing-dependent: the 2.4T/3.2T coherent transition is the ASP event, it is dated 2027-2028, and COHR is named on both the EML and transceiver legs plus the 400G D-EML first-mover claim. None of it is quantified for COHR.** ⚠️ **ASPs are FUNDA estimates, not quoted prices.**
- ⚠️ **Risks GF itself lists, unadorned: "1) AI demand slowdown; 2) supply constraint, 3) competition."** Cross-read: [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md), [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md). - **🔴 THE LTA DISCLOSURE, AND THE COHR-vs-LITE ASYMMETRY IS THE WHOLE POINT.** *"**Coherent: Also signed multi-year LTAs for pump lasers and, notably, 'a large percentage of the biz is now covered by long-term agreements,' INCLUDING TRANSCEIVERS.** This suggests a shift in customer procurement strategies."* Against that: *"**Lumentum: Has LTAs across most of its portfolio, EXCLUDING transceivers** (though hints at some transceiver LTAs for other players). Negotiated **incremental pricing concessions in exchange for long-term commitments**, but still sees price upside."* ➜ **This is the first evidence on this page that the memory-style LTA architecture — durability bought with price upside — is migrating into OPTICS, and that COHR has taken it further than its closest comparable. It is a direct answer to the "is optical a component cycle or a contracted franchise" question, and it cuts in COHR's favour on visibility. ⚠️ It cuts the OTHER way on near-term ASP: LITE is explicitly described as trading price concessions for duration, and COHR's broader coverage implies it has made the same trade over more of its book. Watch reported ASP/GM, not backlog, for the cost of it.**
- **Backlog, sector-wide:** *"Optical demand is **very strong across the board**. Many optical companies are reporting **backlogs stretching into 2028**."* ⚠️ **Unattributed to a specific company and therefore not adopted as a COHR-specific figure — logged as the analyst's characterisation of the group.**
- **🔴 CPO IS NOT DELAYED, AND THE PAGE NOW HAS A DATED CONSENSUS: "There is a broad consensus among industry players that Co-Packaged Optics (CPO) is NOT delayed, with CALENDAR YEAR 2028 expected to be the material year for its ramp."** ➜ **This directly corroborates the BTG/"Kathy" rebuttal of the SemiAnalysis CPO push-out piece already logged on the [[LITE]] page (2026-08-13) and the LITE CEO's "no delay in CPO" at the JPM forum the same day. - **⚠️ MPO IS THE NET-NEW ARCHITECTURE ON THIS PAGE AND IT BROADENS THE CUSTOMER SET AWAY FROM ONE ACCOUNT.** *"**MPO is seen as ADDITIVE to the CPO TAM**, broadening the ecosystem beyond [[NVDA]]… While the CPO ecosystem has largely been centered around Nvidia, **MPO's emergence indicates a broadening of customer interest, with other players (including hyperscalers) pursuing this opportunity, and even Nvidia looking at it.**"* Content and timing: *"**Content is largely similar between CPO and MPO** from a supplier ecosystem perspective"*; the two flavours are **(a) ELS (External Laser Source) — "similar to CPO, early indication for ramp in H2 2027"**, and **(b) embedded — laser packaged near the optical engine, ramping 2028, using a MEDIUM-powered laser (lower ASP than ultra-high-power but higher than 200G EML), "viewed as having a HIGHER BARRIER TO ENTRY."** ⚠️ **The ELS/embedded split is described on this page from LITE's perspective; it is logged here because the content read is stated to be common across the ecosystem. Do not attribute the ASP ladder to COHR without confirmation.**
- **⚠️ THE MARGIN VERDICT IS THE ONE PLACE THE CALL IS NOT FLATTERING TO COHR, AND IT IS EXPLICIT:** *"**Lumentum is expected to see MORE SIGNIFICANT gross margin expansion in FY2027** due to **earlier program ramps (CPO, OCS) and favorable mix. Coherent's margin improvement is tied to 6-inch Indium Phosphide migration and 1.6T ramps.**"* The summary's own tagging: **BULL (Lumentum) / NEUTRAL (Coherent).** ➜ **This is a relative-preference statement inside the pair, and it is the mirror image of the 08-10 Morgan Stanley call already on this page, which PREFERRED COHR to LITE into the prints. Both are now on the page. The distinguishing variable is timing: MS's preference was about the print; JPM's is about the FY27 margin slope, and it rests on COHR's improvement being back-ended behind a manufacturing transition (6-inch InP) rather than a product ramp.** Cross-ref the 6-inch cost framing already carried from the FQ4 call transcript.
- **🔴 A COMPETITIVE ITEM AIMED AT COHR FROM THE [[LITE]] SIDE: "Lumentum is actively pursuing the MERCHANT CW LASER market, driven by internal manufacturing efficiencies. This is expected to be a meaningful contribution in 2027 (10-20% of chip output) with ACCRETIVE gross margins, and is providing NEGOTIATION LEVERAGE across their portfolio, including EML pricing."** ➜ **Read this against the high-power/CW laser shortage thread this page and the LITE/[[AAOI]] pages have carried since 08-09. **FQ1 FY27 guide — well ahead on every line: revenue $2.30B vs Street $2.14–2.15B (~+7%), non-GAAP GM 41.5% vs Street 40.0% (+150bps vs Street), non-GAAP EPS $1.95 vs Street $1.77–1.79 (~+10%).**

- **🔴 THE HEADLINE TARGET, AND IT IS THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT NUMBER ON THE CALL: "having achieved our first $2 billion revenue quarter, we now expect to achieve our first quarter with OVER $3 BILLION OF REVENUE BY THE END OF FISCAL '27"** (Anderson). Raymond James' Simon Leopold called it *"well above current consensus"* on the call itself. ➜ **That is ~+50% from the FQ1 guide midpoint inside four quarters, and it is the framing device for every other disclosure below.**
- **FY26 in full (net-new — the page carried only the quarter):** revenue **$7.12B, +23% reported / +28% pro forma**, the first $7B year in company history; non-GAAP **GM 39.4%, +152bps**; non-GAAP **OM 20.5% (from 17.8%)**; non-GAAP **EPS $5.61, +59%** — *"more than twice the rate of revenue growth."* Non-GAAP opex **$1.35B (from $1.17B)**, **19.0% of revenue (from 20.1%)**. **Q4 non-GAAP opex $377M (18.4% of revenue) vs $348M prior / $307M a year ago; R&D 10.2% of revenue; SG&A 8.2% (from 10.3% a year ago).**
- **🔴 THE MARGIN TRACK RECORD, STATED AS A STREAK: "in EIGHT OUT OF THE PAST NINE QUARTERS we have increased our gross margin… the accumulation of improvement is OVER 660 BASIS POINTS"** (Luther). On the **>42% Investor Day target**: *"we're still early… I do view that we are early in our strategy for gross margin expansion. But we are extremely focused on getting to greater than 42%… **once we get to our target of greater than 42%, we will no doubt RAISE THE TARGET**."* Her four named drivers, all still ahead: the bulk of the 6-inch InP ramp, new product ramps (1.6T, OCS, CPO, multi-rail, thermal), cost reduction, pricing optimisation. ⚠️ **She also pre-empts the flow-through question: at the FQ1 midpoint opex is ALREADY BELOW the 18%-of-revenue target model given only last year — "there's more operating leverage that we'll get out of R&D as well."**
- **🔴 THE 6-INCH DATAPOINT THE PAGE HAS BEEN WAITING FOR, AND IT IS A UNIT NUMBER RATHER THAN A CAPACITY CLAIM: "we produced about 80% MORE INDIUM PHOSPHIDE LASERS in our June quarter than we did the prior year… those are the lasers that go specifically into our 800G and 1.6T transceivers. And so we would expect our DATA CENTER GROWTH THIS QUARTER ON A YEAR-OVER-YEAR BASIS TO EXCEED 80% — we've certainly got the lasers this quarter to make that happen"** (Anderson). ➜ **A company-stated floor of >80% y/y data-center growth for the September quarter, derived from lasers already produced. That is the most concrete forward datapoint on the page and it is mechanically supported rather than modelled.** Ramp status: **internal InP output doubling y/y by the end of the current quarter — one quarter ahead of the original plan — then MORE THAN DOUBLING AGAIN by the end of calendar '27, with substrates and critical inputs already secured; "given the strength of customer demand, we are planning additional capacity BEYOND 2027."** Texas and Sweden are in 6-inch production now (**EMLs, CW lasers and photodiodes, all yielding better than the 3-inch lines**); **Zurich comes online 1H CY27**.
- **⚠️ AND THE CFO CORRECTED THE MECHANISM, WHICH MATTERS BECAUSE THIS PAGE HAS BEEN CARRYING IT AS A YIELD STORY: "in terms of 6-inch yields… it's 6-INCH COST STRUCTURE, because we get FOUR TIMES the amount of output from that wafer versus 3-inch — four times, but at HALF THE COST. So it's that cost structure that's beneficial to us"** (Luther), with Anderson adding that 6-inch yields are *higher* than 3-inch but *"again, it's more the benefit of the 6-inch is the COST STRUCTURE benefit."* ➜ **Read against Aurelion's 2.25x usable-area argument and Citi's 4.36x chips-per-wafer: management's own framing is 4x output at half the cost = ~8x cost-per-die improvement, and it is explicitly NOT a yield-gap story.**

We have that capacity available. We're really just constrained by the ramp of the indium phosphide production. It's really as simple as that"** (Anderson, to Needham's Ryan Kuntz). ⚠️ **This closes a question the page had open — the bottleneck is upstream of assembly/test, so incremental InP output converts to revenue almost immediately.** Corollary he volunteers: **no near-term external sales of InP lasers** — *"our data center transceiver demand is absorbing every bit of capacity that we have and then some."*

**🆕🔴 2026-08-11 — PRINTS TOMORROW (WED 2026-08-12 AMC), AND [[LITE]] JUST SET THE BAR: LITE beat on every line with a September guide ~8% above Street and the stock went nowhere. Two new read-throughs arrived for COHR, one bullish on structural share and one bullish on the market itself.**
- **🔴🔴 THE STRUCTURAL SHARE ARGUMENT — CORRECTED 2026-08-13 FROM THE RAW TRANSCRIPT, AND THE CORRECTION MAKES IT A CLEANER COHR BULL THAN THE PAGE PREVIOUSLY CARRIED (Papa Sylla, buy-side call relayed 2026-08-11).** The 21h pass read the speaker's *"light"* as **Lumentum**; it is **Innolight**, the Chinese transceiver maker the proposed US restriction would hit. The prior wording is preserved in `## Changelog` (2026-08-13). What he actually argued:
  - **Innolight is ~35–40% of the transceiver market**, so a ban *"severely increases the supply-demand imbalance"* and NVDA/hyperscalers push back — which is why he rates a full ban unlikely and calls the chatter **"tactical negotiation."** China's retaliation lever is the **~70% of InP feedstock it controls**, aimed at the *input* to Western laser makers.
  - **🔴 The de-risking number runs the other way from how it was logged: hyperscalers cutting their allocation to INNOLIGHT from 40% to 30% over two years is a SHARE TAILWIND to COHR and [[LITE]], not a leak away from LITE.** *"They want to de-risk from China. So that is positive."*
  - **🔴 AND THE ASYMMETRY THAT ACTUALLY FAVOURS COHR, WHICH WAS LOST ENTIRELY: "for Coherent I totally agree, Coherent in particular would benefit from this… but for Lumentum, most of their business is still the LASER, not the transceiver. [Innolight] is a big customer — probably their biggest customer in the laser. So if [Innolight leaves] the market, you are hoping Coherent absorbs all of those lasers quickly as well. So for Lumentum it's NOT AS GOOD as for Coherent."** ➜ **A ban costs LITE its largest merchant EML buyer while handing COHR transceiver share. He cites the 08-04 tape as the market already pricing this: COHR up 10%+ vs LITE up 5–7% (actual prints COHR +12% / LITE +9%).** His only bound: **if China retaliates on lasers, "everybody loses."** ⚠️ **This is the mechanism behind MS's 08-04 ranking (COHR clearest beneficiary) and Jefferies' 08-07 ("bullish for COHR, less so LITE") — both of which asserted the conclusion without supplying the reason. Third-party mark on the target: Innolight ~27% of the global DC optical-transceiver market with ~90% of revenue earned outside China (Damnang / "The Optical Edge", 2026-08-04).**
- **🔴 THE SAME CALL, PART 2 — THE BUY-SIDE BOGEY SHEET FOR COHR'S OWN PRINT, PUBLISHED THE DAY BEFORE IT AND NET-NEW TO THIS PAGE (Papa Sylla, 2026-08-11). Score it against the actual print above.** *"For Coherent, the expectation is LOWER in terms of beat versus Lumentum. They never do [beat as much]."* His numbers: **June ~$1.9B revenue / ~$1.50 EPS, September ~$2.1B / ~$1.70 EPS — "approximately 5% of beat… I think that should be enough."** ➜ **ACTUAL: June $2.05B / $1.74; September guided $2.3B (mid) / $1.95 (mid). COHR beat his bogey on all four legs, by ~8% on June revenue and ~10% on the September guide — and still traded −5%.** ⚠️ **That is the cleanest available proof that the −5% was a whisper/positioning outcome, not a delivery outcome.** **What he wanted qualitatively: "the key focus is the guidance, the GROSS MARGIN into the September quarter… they want to see the impact of more [integrated] product, like the CPO coming in… but the SIX-INCH FAB is where there's still a lot of question."** On six-inch he relays a positive channel check: after an Asia NDR *"they sound really good… now we are fully past all of the hiccup on six-inch, yields are good"* — so **"the September [guide] should reflect that in terms of having a really good gross-margin expansion from the June quarter."** ⚠️ **Against a 40.5% midpoint guide (vs 40.2% actual), that expectation was only partly met — which is a better explanation of the tape than "Industrial was soft."** **His FY28 revenue frame: "around 25, 25–30" ($25–30bn) for fiscal 28 — far above the Capstone model's $19.6bn CY28E and above any sell-side number on this page. And on margin: "some investors expect them to potentially [go] beyond the 41% [gross margin], and longer term they believe they can get to 40, 45 to 50 IF THEY REALLY OUTPERFORM."** _Buy-side call summary, not research; treat the figures as the speaker's estimates._
- **The market-level read-through, and it is directly in COHR's coherent-optics franchise (DIGITIMES research, 2026-08-11):** *"**scale-across, which is essentially a data center interconnect (DCI), is gradually becoming the core of the next wave of AI infrastructure**"* — hyperscalers distributing GPU clusters across campuses **2–20 km apart** linked by high-speed optics, because a single site is capped by power, floor space and cooling. **"As AI interconnect bandwidth rises to 800G and 1.6T, traditional IMDD is increasingly insufficient for high-capacity, long-distance transmission, making COHERENT OPTICS the mainstream technology for scale-across."** The cost answer being engineered: **"coherent lite"** (simplified DSPs and optical specs to cut module cost, targeting 1.6T campus DCI) and **"multi-rail"** (higher line-system density, shared cabinets/power/cooling across rails, fewer repeater huts). ⚠️ **The report names "[[MRVL]], [[CIEN]] and others" as leading the scale-across optical supply chain — COHR is not named in the excerpt available, so treat this as TAM/architecture evidence rather than a COHR share claim.** A companion DIGITIMES piece headlines *"demand for coherent optics supply chain heats up."*
- **⚠️ THE SET-UP RISK IS NOW EXPLICIT, and LITE demonstrated it hours earlier.** LITE printed rev/GM/OM/EPS all above consensus **and** guided September revenue ~8% and EPS ~16% above Street — **and traded flat-to-down**, because the intraday buy-side whisper had drifted to a $1.3B guide. JPM's verdict: *"the tepid response feels like an **expectations problem**… reminiscent of the TXN print"* (JPM / Joshua Meyers, 2026-08-11). **Both names had also fallen hard on Monday — LITE/COHR -9%/-14% on the FCC/China-ban speculation, with Jefferies' Edison view being that the ban is unlikely (Jefferies / Rankie Wong, 2026-08-11, who notes he "didn't come across any good reasons tbh" for the crash).** ➜ **COHR reports into a group where beating consensus has just been shown to be insufficient. The bar is the whisper.**
- **Broker logistics for tomorrow: JPM hosts a post-earnings COHR/LITE analyst callback with Joe Cardoso on Fri 2026-08-14 10am ET; MS hosts a COHR/LITE/[[CSCO]] 2Q group callback with Meta Marshall on 08-13 8am ET; JPM also carried COHR in its "Chips for Breakfast" Buyside Bars set for this week's prints** (JPM / Joshua Meyers; MS TMT desk; both 2026-08-11).

**🆕 2026-08-10 (PM) — prints Wed 2026-08-12; MS prefers COHR to [[LITE]] into the pair, and Japan just de-risked the demand signal.**
- **MS on the pair (Morgan Stanley / Meta Marshall, via Amir Amerian, 2026-08-10):** buyside expectations *"have become balanced"* and she previews both slightly cautious — but **"she sees MORE MARGIN UPSIDE FOR COHR in this set of results."** ⚠️ Her caveat applies to both names: *"this set of results **will not solve the longer-term debates** on the stocks, namely **execution on CPO ramp and durability of margins**,"* and results will hinge on *"**conventional pluggable capacity, yields and customer deployments**"* more than on architecture narrative. Both managements are expected to lean on **NPO rather than CPO**.
- **🔴 Japan's optical earnings season ended on a strong note, and the guide-raise cadence is the datapoint (Morgan Stanley desk / Amir Amerian, 2026-08-10).** **Fujikura** reported **1Q OP 42% above consensus** and **raised FY OP to ¥432bn (cons ¥330bn, MSRe ¥345bn)** — *"it again raised… after raising its guide above-cons in June"*, i.e. **two guide hikes in under two months** — explicitly on *"**higher probability of winning large-scale projects in FY 2H**."* Stock **+~14% Friday, +7% overnight**. **Furukawa Electric** reported **1Q OP 50% above consensus** and raised **FY OP to ¥123bn (cons ¥101bn)** on *"wider sales of DC-related products in Optical Solutions and Digital Infrastructure Components"*; **+~12%** and holding. **Sumitomo Electric** missed in Information & Communication on a **company-specific capacity-expansion downtime issue**, not demand.
- **Supply-chain corroboration from the test/materials layer:** **Anritsu** (KEYS peer) reported a **large 1Q orders beat from optical transceiver testing demand**; **Misumi revised up its F3/27 China Telecom revenue by 48%** (read: China transceiver); **HOYA** Optical Solutions revenue **+22% JPY / +11% y/y cc to ¥16.5bn**, citing **CUPO polarization glass for optical transceivers** as the primary growth driver.
- **⚠ The regulatory kicker, and it is still unpublished rule-making (MS desk, 2026-08-04, carried forward):** reports that **the Trump administration and FCC are preparing restrictions on new Chinese data-center components, with optical transceivers specifically mentioned** — *"no formal rule has been published, but… a rule could go into effect this year."* Tape reaction on the report: **COHR +12%, FN +16%, LITE +8% vs [[AXTI]] −5%.** Marshall reads it as positive for non-China transceiver suppliers *"as there isn't enough supply from non-Chinese companies to meet global AI capex needs"*, and notes **both LITE and COHR have publicly advocated for tighter controls**. ⚠️ The desk's own hedge is the right one to carry: *"let's see if this is posturing into the Trump-Xi September meeting."*

**Q3 FY26 (2026-05-06):** Record revenue of $1.8B, +7% q/q and +21% y/y reported; **+9% q/q and +27% y/y pro forma** (ex-A&D and the divested Munich division). Non-GAAP gross margin **39.6%**, +57bp q/q and +105bp y/y; non-GAAP operating margin 20.3%; non-GAAP EPS **$1.41, +55% y/y**. DC&Comms +>40% y/y; within it, data center +13% q/q / +37% y/y (second consecutive quarter of double-digit sequential growth) and communications +16% q/q / **+60% y/y** on DCI/scale-across. Industrial down modestly but semicap bookings inflecting. Backlog at a record; **orders now extend into CY2028 and LTAs to end of decade**; Q2 FY26 data center book-to-bill exceeded 4x (2026-02-04).

Balance sheet de-levered hard: net debt leverage **0.5x**, down from 1.7x in Q2 and 2.1x a year ago; cash jumped to $3B after **NVIDIA's $2B equity investment** (announced 2026-03-02). Capex stepped up to $290M (vs $154M prior) and guided higher again — pure capacity-add. **Q4 FY26 guide:** revenue $1.91–2.05B, GM 39–41%, non-GAAP EPS **$1.52–1.72** — an implied acceleration. Anderson framed the June quarter as "a new inflection point" and reiterated FY27 growth > FY26.

Operational drivers: 6-inch InP ramp running ahead of plan — internal InP capacity to double by CY-end (now a quarter early), then **more than double again by CY27** (a ~4x over two years). 6-inch yields exceed 3-inch on all three device types; 6-inch produces >4x devices at <half the cost (Sherman TX, Järfälla Sweden, + new Zurich site for early CY27). First 6-inch-content transceivers shipped in Q3, already aiding revenue and GM. 800G to grow in CY26 with 1.6T ramping rapidly (EML + SiPh first, 200G VCSEL-based later) — Coherent (as II-VI) began 1.6T mass shipment in 2Q25, in line with Chinese leaders Innolight/TFC (Bernstein China Next Winners, 2025-10-27). OCS TAM raised to **>$4B**, bottleneck cleared, ramping across two sites. CPO TAM put at **>$15B** ("conservative"), initial scale-out revenue H2 CY26, scale-up H2 CY27; JPM independently sizes the scale-up CPO laser/light-source TAM at **>$9B** for the Broadcom/Coherent/Lumentum group, and views scale-up CPO (vs scale-out, which likely stays pluggable) as the more probable near-term adoption path — a greenfield opportunity rather than a displacement risk (JPM Hardware & Networking, 2025-12-15).

- **InP ramp / margin path (incremental).** Coherent is guiding **a quarter ahead on its 2x InP ramp for cal-26**, then **another 2x in cal-27**; callback messaging is **high-teens data-center revenue growth for the June quarter** with no pushback on further acceleration into FY27 (something around **30%+ FY27 revenue growth**); **6-inch becomes >50% of total InP volume from the September quarter**, when it starts to materially contribute to gross margin (JPM "Networking recap", 2026-05-08). Coherent affirms a path to **>42% gross margin** via 1.6T mix, 6-inch wafers ramping, more internal sourcing, and industrial-side pricing — a series of small levers, not one (BofA CIEN supply-chain call, 2026-06-12).
- **6-inch yield edge vs LITE.** Coherent reports **every single product on 6-inch — EMLs, CW lasers, photodiodes — is yielding better than the prior generation**, not just in aggregate; the divergence vs Lumentum's more hesitant 6-inch stance is partly base-rate (Coherent's prior-gen yields were lower, so relative improvement is larger) (BofA CIEN supply-chain call, 2026-06-12). Demand visibility is "quite strong" with **limited double-ordering** from customers (BofA CIEN supply-chain call, 2026-06-12).
- **NVDA ELS read-through.** Because Lumentum still sounds hesitant on selling the full ELS package to NVDA, the likely medium-term outcome is **NVDA securing Coherent and Fabrinet as its two ELS suppliers** (BofA CIEN supply-chain call, 2026-06-12). As a **large merchant customer of Lumentum's lasers**, Coherent's order behavior is a demand tell — it is "asking for more, not less" (Hurlston, JPM LITE mgmt call, 2026-05-07).
- **Citi debuts an InP supply model — supply stays below demand through 2028; COHR the 6-inch leader, Buy $420 reiterated (Citi "InP Supply Constraint Will Likely Persist," Papa Sylla, 2026-07-16):** Citi's new InP (EML + CW) supply model, paired with its China team's optical-demand model, shows **supply remaining below demand for the next three years** despite rapid US + Chinese capacity adds — total industry laser-chip supply modeled to nearly triple from **~280M chips (2025) to ~1.1B (2028E, ~+60% CAGR)**, with the **6-inch transition the largest structural lever (a 6-inch wafer yields ~4.36x more chips than 3-inch)**. **EML supply runs ~33% below demand in 2026, narrowing to 20% (2027) and 11% (2028)**; **CW is less constrained in 2026 but the gap widens to 19% (2027) / 55% (2028)** as higher-power CPO/NPO CW demand and lower high-power yields bite. **100G/lane EML falls from ~55% of EML wafer allocation (2025) to ~5% (2028)** as the market moves to 800G/1.6T. **COHR-specific:** it is framed as **the leader in the 6-inch transition and the most vertically-internal supplier vs Chinese competition**; at OFC 2026 it showed a multi-tech 1.6T approach (SiPho PICs, 400mW InP CW for CPO/SiPho pluggables, 200G InP EML, 200G GaAs VCSEL), with the 1.6T ramp led by EML + SiPh and **VCSEL-based 1.6T in H2 CY26**, a differential-EML (D-EML) path to 400G/lane for 3.2T+, and **single-mode CPO (high-power InP CW + SiPh) targeting mass production H2 CY26**. Citi keeps COHR at **Buy, PT $420 (unchanged; price $299.38; EPS $5.47/$9.78 nc)**, calling the recent pullback a buying opportunity; the swing on COHR's share of the >$15B CPO TAM (beyond the NVDA deal) is **whether Broadcom sources CPO components from COHR or builds its own vertically-integrated solution**. (Citi InP note, 2026-07-16)
- **MS "clearest beneficiary" of optical into scale-up; rebuilds COHR model for CPO % (MS "Bringing More Light to Scale-Up Networks — Updated Scale-Up Primer", Meta Marshall / Joseph Moore, 2026-07-13):** MS sizes the scale-up market at **~$70bn by 2030 (>4x last year's est)**, with copper staying longer but CPO "eventual" (meaningful adoption not until NVDA's Feynman generation — small 2028, stronger 2029+; **OCP in October the next catalyst**), calling recent optical sell-offs "overdone." MS names **GLW/LITE/COHR the clearest beneficiaries of optical moving into scale-up** and rebuilt the COHR model for CPO penetration %. COHR-specific: **broad exposure across the full CPO/NPO component stack** — high-power CW lasers, external laser source modules, FAUs, polarization-maintaining fiber, isolators, thermoelectric coolers — and mgmt has been **the most aggressive on scale-up sizing, describing the opportunity as "orders of magnitude larger than scale-out"**; multi-architecture support (InP, silicon photonics, VCSEL) positions COHR to win across NPO / 2D CPO / 2.5D CPO. Model tweaks: **2027E rev $9,463M→$9,438M, 2028E rev $12,621M→$12,863M (+2%)**; FY28 adj/core-EPS sensitivity spans **~$10.8-$12.65** across the scale-out × scale-up CPO-adoption grid. (MS "Updated Scale-Up Primer", 2026-07-13)

- **A competitor's-management view of COHR, plus the clearest third-party framing yet of why the 6-inch line is the competitive weapon (Aurelion Research/PhotonCap "A Conversation with Lumentum", 2026-07-29 — joint independent publication; management meeting + investment analysis by Aurelion Research, technical comments by PhotonCap).** **What Lumentum's own VP of IR said about Coherent, in her framing: "Lumentum and Coherent are the two companies left standing from telecom," an industry she describes as historically punishing to serve — and "Coherent is stronger at the systems level and is working to catch up on the component side"** (Lumentum mgmt via Aurelion Research, 2026-07-29). ⚠️ **Read that as a competitor's characterisation, not an independent assessment — but note it concedes COHR the systems-level edge while claiming the component moat, which is exactly the split this page already carries from JPM/Citi.** **Aurelion's own investment analysis makes COHR's 6-inch line its #1 bear argument against Lumentum, and it is the most explicit statement of the mechanism on this page: "By switching from 4 inch to 6 inch wafers, Coherent produces roughly 2.25 times the usable area per wafer, yielding more laser die at a lower cost per die. Lumentum still runs 4 inch wafers and is trying to catch up. If Coherent's 6 inch yield closes the gap on Lumentum's 4 inch performance, this deeply undermines Lumentum's pricing power."** The bite is that **Lumentum framed its own competitive advantage as bits per square area of indium phosphide, "so a competitor with more than twice the usable area per wafer makes direct headway on that metric."** Aurelion also states **"Coherent's indium phosphide lasers are also sold out through 2027"** — consistent with the orders-into-CY2028 / LTAs-to-end-of-decade framing already on this page. **⚠️ Note the 2.25x figure is a 4-inch→6-inch area ratio and is NOT the same number as Citi's 4.36x chips/wafer (07-16), which is measured off a 3-inch base — both stand.** **PhotonCap adds the second-sourcing evidence, and it reads as a COHR positive: "second sourcing is in progress, not a hypothesis. NVIDIA's technical blog names three ELS vendors, Lumentum, Sumitomo, and Coherent. The same day Lumentum got its $2B, Coherent got an identical $2B. Coherent's OFC 2026 release formalizes its 6 inch InP ramp and a 400mW CW laser, and AAOI announced sampling of a 400mW narrow linewidth laser in December 2025. The time axis is what matters. Competing capacity lands outside the FY27 LTA window"** (PhotonCap technical comment, 2026-07-29). **The named ELS trio (LITE / Sumitomo / COHR) is net-new to this page** — Sumitomo has appeared here only as an InP substrate/capacity name, not as a named NVDA external-light-source vendor. The **400mW CW laser at OFC 2026** already sits on this page via Citi's 07-16 note; this is independent corroboration.
- **Channel numbers on the CW/CPO ramp that name Coherent alongside Lumentum (Aurelion Research, 2026-07-29, citing Global Semi Research's 2026-06-10 response to the SemiAnalysis note):** supply-chain checks put **NVIDIA's guidance to Lumentum *and Coherent* for high-power continuous-wave lasers at roughly 40MM units in January, rising to roughly 100MM by April and May**, which back-solves to **CPO switch shipments of roughly 200,000-300,000 in 2027 scaling toward 600,000-800,000 in 2028**, and lines up with **TSMC's COUPE** plan. ⚠️ **Aurelion's own weighting: "These are channel figures rather than company disclosure, so we weight them accordingly."** Set against the bear it was written to rebut: **SemiAnalysis's 2026-06-09 institutional note argued scale-out CPO shipments would be revised down through FY27 with scale-up pushed to FY29, on the grounds that CPO output is driven by a single, very limited ASIC and that ASIC integration is difficult with challenging underlying economics** — the specific mechanism behind the ~11% 2026-06-10 drawdown already logged on this page. **PhotonCap's split of that risk is the useful part for COHR's mix: an integration-driven delay "pushes CW and ELSFP revenue out while extending the EML cycle," because demand "stays in pluggables longer, and pluggables are EML intensive"; only a broader AI-capex slowdown hurts both architectures.**

## Capstone estimates (house model)
_Source: Capstone official model (`Modelo COHR.xlsx`, 2026-05-26). Built on Jefferies/Blayne Curtis._

| | CY24 | CY25 | CY26E | CY27E | CY28E |
|---|--:|--:|--:|--:|--:|
| Revenue ($bn) | 5.3 | 6.3 | 9.1 | 16.6 | 19.6 |
| EPS — pro forma ($) | 2.76 | 4.35 | 8.27 | 19.21 | 23.60 |
| Gross margin | ~37% | ~39% | ~40% | ~41% | ~41% |

**House read:** revenue $6.3bn → $19.6bn (CY25-28E) on the 800G/1.6T ramp (DC/Comms 68% → 88% of mix); pro forma EPS $4.35 → $23.60. (Capstone official model — Jefferies-based, 2026-05-26)

## Debate / thesis

**🔴 2026-08-17 (INGEST) — THE SHARPEST ENGINEERING BEAR CASE THIS PAGE HAS EVER CARRIED, AND WHAT MAKES IT UNUSUAL IS THAT IT CONTRADICTS NOTHING THE COMPANY HAS DISCLOSED — BECAUSE THE COMPANY HAS DISCLOSED NO YIELD DATA. THE ASYMMETRY *IS* THE FINDING. And in the same window, an outside analyst places COHR's InP ramp AHEAD of [[LITE]]'s. Both are on the page; the disagreement is the signal.**

_(Source: **Irrational Analysis**, "Coherent Q4 FY26 Earnings — The opposite of Lumentum", 2026-08-13. ⚠️ **SOURCE CLASS, and read it before weighting anything below: an opinionated independent Substack written by a working engineer, self-declared LONG SEMIS. It carries no rating, no PT and no estimates. The ENGINEERING CLAIMS are the content; the invective is not, and is not reproduced.** Local copy: [IA_coherent.html](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/IA_coherent.html).)_

- 🔴 **THE THESIS, AND IT IS A MARGIN-ACCOUNTING ARGUMENT DRESSED AS AN ENGINEERING ONE: *"Coherent's call was crap and so were their numbers… The opposite of Lumentum."* The author argues the "higher revenue mix is transceivers" explanation does NOT account for the gross-margin gap versus [[LITE]], and that **the only way COHR's numbers make sense is if CPO-laser YIELD is bad.*** ⚠️ **This is an inference from the P&L, not from data — he says so by demanding the data. But note where it lands on this page: the FQ1 FY27 synthesis already records that the guide midpoint came in at **40.5%** against a buy-side want of **41–41.5%**, and the [[LITE]] comparison is exactly the one MS used to prefer COHR into the prints (08-03, 08-10). The bear supplies a MECHANISM for that miss which the sell-side notes did not.**
- 🔴🔴 **WHAT MAKES IT UNFALSIFIABLE, AND WHY THAT IS THE POINT: COHR DISCLOSES NO CPO-LASER YIELD DATA.** The author's repeated demand is *"YIELD EML AT WHAT DATARATE? YIELD CW LASER AT WHAT POWER CLASS?"*, and he notes an **MS analyst asked the leading version of the same question on the call** — *"is your miniscule gross margin expansion [vs Lumentum] because of mix shift or 6in yield?"* His challenge: **Jim Anderson could publish the same plots [[LITE]] published; the author predicts he will not.** ➤ **The honest framing for this page: the note CONTRADICTS NOTHING COHR HAS SAID, because COHR has said nothing measurable on this axis. Every yield claim here — including management's own 08-12 framing that the 6-inch benefit is "cost structure (4x output at half the cost), NOT a yield gap" — is an assertion against an assertion. That asymmetry is the finding, and it is a REAL disclosure gap at a company whose bull case rests on a manufacturing advantage.** ⚠️ It also revives, from a second source, the **Irrational Analysis 6-inch yield skepticism of 2026-05-08** that the `Sinal` table still records as **unresolved**.
- ⚠️ **THE 6-INCH InP HYPOTHESIS, AND IT IS EXCULPATORY RATHER THAN ACCUSATORY — which is why it is worth carrying:** *"Intuitively, 6-inch InP production should be higher yield. The tools are much better. New-generation Aixtron MOCVD reactors (for 6in) are higher performance. Instead of using ancient lithography tools from no-name companies, you can use a semi-modern ASML tool. So…. why is their yield bad?"* His hypothesis: **the 6-inch InP WAFERS themselves may have bad uniformity** — *"the best supplier ([[AXTI]]) does not have 6[-inch] wafers. That is still under R&D"* — so **"Coherent's yield problems might not be their fault. Maybe it's SUMITOMO's fault."** ➤ **Two consequences. (1) It relocates the bear from COHR's execution to its SUPPLIER, which is a materially less damaging version and a testable one. (2) It creates an explicit opening for AXT once it ships 6-inch — filed to [[AXTI]] as a product-roadmap datapoint. ⚠️ Note the tension with what this page already carries: COHR's own AXT agreement (signed 2026-06-26, $22.3m prepayment) is specifically for "the DEVELOPMENT AND SUPPLY OF 6-INCH WAFERS" — i.e. COHR is already funding the second source this argument implies it needs.** Explicitly self-labelled a conspiracy theory by its own author.
- ⚠️ **THE VCSEL OBJECTION, AND IT IS THE ONE ITEM THAT ATTACKS A PRODUCT RATHER THAN A MARGIN: on VCSEL-based NPO/CPO the optimal data rate is 32G or 64G NRZ, and "200G PAM4 on a VCSEL WILL NOT WORK from a reliability perspective… going to FAIL GR-468."** Even if it passed reliability, he argues **energy efficiency suffers** (driver complexity, FEC needed for crosstalk, and the burden thrown onto Rx TIA sensitivity). He also asks whether **"PhotonLink"** — the platform COHR launches 09-21 and which the FQ1 FY27 synthesis lists as a genuinely net-new BULL item — **is the brand name for COHR's VCSEL CPO/NPO offering.** ✅ **He concedes COHR "has great VCSELs, second only to Broadcom."** ➤ **CONDITIONAL, and the condition is the whole item: if PhotonLink is VCSEL-based at 200G PAM4, this is a direct technical objection to the page's newest bull item and GR-468 qualification is a falsifiable, dated test rather than an opinion. If it is not VCSEL-based, the objection lapses. The product's platform, lane rate and modulation format are the things to establish at the 09-21 launch — added to `## Catalysts`.**
- ✅ **AND THE COUNTERWEIGHT, FROM THE SAME WINDOW AND POINTING THE OTHER WAY: on Nokia's earnings call an analyst compared the InP ramp to COHR, "ALREADY DOING PRETTY WELL," and placed [[LITE]] "A BIT BEHIND"** (FUNDA, "Deep|Optics: The Scale-Across TAM Is Taking Shape", 2026-08-12). ⚠️ **Thin provenance — an analyst aside on a third company's call, relayed by an independent newsletter — and it addresses the InP RAMP rather than CPO-laser yield, so it does not refute the bear above. But it is the third-party ranking that sits closest to it in time and it points the opposite way, and it is consistent with what this page already carries from the competitor's own page: Aurelion makes COHR's 6-inch line its **#1 bear argument against LITE** ("~2.25x the usable area per wafer… Lumentum still runs 4 inch wafers and is trying to catch up," 07-29).** ➤ **KEEP BOTH. An engineer infers bad CPO-laser yield from the margin line; an analyst covering the customer says the InP ramp is going well and better than LITE's. They are not the same metric — wafer-size/output ramp versus per-device yield at a power class — and until COHR publishes yield data neither can be closed. The disagreement is the alpha; the resolution is a disclosure request, not another opinion.**
- **Also from the transceiver-ban memo, and it is a clean net-positive read for COHR (Irrational Analysis, "[Market Memo] Orange Man Attempted Chinese Transceiver Ban", 2026-08-04):** *"Whatever happens, this is good for Lumentum, Coherent, and AAOI."* Mechanics that matter here: **the restriction would hit NEW MODELS ONLY** — *"everyone has already qualed all the models so this becomes issue at 3.2T which is far away"* — which BOUNDS the near-term upside this page logged from the 08-04 tape (COHR +12%) and is consistent with management calling the reports *"speculative"* on 08-12. **COHR is named as a Tower ([[TSEM]]) SiPho-PIC customer alongside [[LITE]] and [[AAOI]], so an EML→SiPho production pivot still lands at Tower**; and the author's central claim is that **a SiPho transceiver has ~50% LESS InP content than an EML-based one but significantly HIGHER active-alignment content**, with the pivot itself *"trivial… The issue is active alignment capacity/throughput, not the design itself."* ⚠️ **For COHR specifically that is more benign than for [[LITE]] — COHR's hybrid InP+SiPh model is the version JPM already calls "structurally defensive against InP-capacity overbuild" (2025-12-15) — but it does erode the InP-vertical-integration premium at the margin, and it points the value at an assembly step where the author says Chinese module makers are an order of magnitude better than the West. Cross-ref [[TSEM]], [[AAOI]], [[AXTI]], [themes/china-export](themes/china-export.md).**
- **🆕 Fabrinet read-through, relevant to COHR's SYSTEMS-customer mix (Fabrinet Q4 FY26 earnings call, 2026-08-17 — FN company primary, Bloomberg initial-draft transcript; ⚠️ no FN wiki page, plain text).** 🔴 **FN's FY26 ≥10% customers are Cisco 20%, NVIDIA 16%, NOKIA 11% (new to the list) and Amazon 11% — and CIENA IS NOT ON IT**, which JPM's Cardoso said on the call surprised him. ➤ **Read it for COHR's systems-customer mix rather than for FN: Nokia crossing 10% at the largest non-Chinese optical CM, with FN saying *"the Infinera business has been rising"* and *"we feel we're just getting started,"* corroborates the Nokia-optical demand thread this page already carries (JPM/Scott Silver on Nokia AI order intake, 07-26; JPM/Cardoso Nokia 2Q26 read-through, 07-23) — while Ciena's absence is a caution against treating the whole systems complex as moving together.** Also directly on the architecture debate: **FN is *"working on CPO today with a handful of customers and we're already building devices, albeit not yet at full-scale volumes,"* and *"NPO, I think probably represents a more NEAR-TERM opportunity than CPO."*** ✅ **That is an independent, manufacturing-side confirmation of the NPO-before-CPO sequencing — which supports management's *"CPO-or-NPO projects at almost every customer"* framing (08-12) without touching the CONTENT-parity dispute the `Sinal` table scores as ✗ CONTESTED. Sequencing corroborated; parity still unevidenced.** Scale for context: **FN Q4 revenue $1.316bn (+45% y/y); data-center revenue $669m, +68% y/y, 51% of total, ECI at a >$1bn annualised run-rate; revenue-capacity ladder raised to $12.5–14bn from ~$11.5bn a quarter ago.** ⚠️ **And the item that cuts against the simple AI-optics read: NVIDIA revenue at FN was DOWN 20%+ for the year** while total grew mid-30s. Full readout on [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md).
- **Capstone model (house view, built on Jefferies/Blayne Curtis):** revenue $6.3B→$9.1B→$16.6B→$19.6B (CY25-28E, +19/+44/+83/+18%) on the 800G/1.6T DC ramp (DC/Comms mix 68%→88%); pro-forma EPS $4.35 / $8.27 / $19.21 / $23.60; GM ~39%→41%, EBITDA margin ~23%→33%. (Capstone official model — based on Jefferies, 2026-05-26)
- **Bull:** Demand "exceptionally strong with no signs of attenuation," visibility into CY2028, multi-year LTAs that bundle customer upfront capex + demand commitments (Anderson, 2026-05-06). InP vertical integration is the chokepoint — China controls 70%+ of global InP output and Coherent is ~1 year early to 6-inch (@ParadisLabs, 2026-06-13; briefing). NVIDIA's $2B equity stake + multi-year CPO supply deal to end of decade validates the franchise; NVDA 13F also disclosed a 7.79M-share holding and Druckenmiller/Duquesne took a stake (briefings, 2026-05-16/18). Multiple incremental growth vectors layering on (OCS, CPO, Multi-Rail ~$2B+ TAM H1 CY27, thermal/Thermadite + TEGs H2 CY27). Margin runway to the **>42% investor-day target** driven by 6-inch cost, yield and pricing optimization (Luther, 2026-05-06; MS, 2026-06-12). **JPM initiated COHR at Buy/OW** (ref $260.64) as a key beneficiary of broad optical growth and ranks it **top of the optical basket — COHR > LITE > CIEN > GLW > FN** — on diversified revenue drivers, "self-help" margin leverage, AI revenue mix lifting from **~22% (CY25) toward 29% then ~41%**, and ~12% revenue growth. The key differentiator vs Lumentum: COHR's **hybrid (InP + SiPh/VCSEL) manufacturing model is structurally defensive against InP-capacity overbuild**, whereas the SiPh shift (cheaper CW lasers displacing higher-ASP EMLs) is a sharper headwind for a pure-InP merchant model; JPM also flags COHR's VCSEL franchise as underappreciated for eventual scale-out adoption (JPM Hardware & Networking, 2025-12-15). COHR is also tagged among the best-positioned names across the DCI/coherent-pluggable, RLS and amplifier opportunities (JPM Hardware & Networking, 2025-12-05). Sell-side PTs: **Citi (Papa Sylla) Buy $420** — highest bull case, FY28E EPS ~$16, +52% vs Street; **Jefferies (Blayne Curtis) Buy $375**; **Barclays (T. O'Malley) OW $350**; **JPM (Samik Chatterjee) OW**, "buy the dip" (2026-05-07/06-11 briefings).
- **Bear:** Coherent is seen as **less in control of its own capacity than Lumentum** — "it's just taken a little bit longer to ramp" (Meta Marshall, MS, 2026-05-07). On a relative-quality view, Lumentum's CPO lasers look better-specified and Coherent's **liquid-crystal OCS is less field-tested** than Lumentum's MEMS arrays (Mike, Rothschild/Redburn, 2026-05-19). Gross-margin fall-through is underwhelming given the 6-inch ramp and exits — incremental GM only ~44% y/y; "margins leave more to be desired" (O'Malley/Barclays; O'Malley question, 2026-05-06). Citi explicitly **prefers LITE over COHR on a 12-month view** on faster growth (LITE +42% FY26 sales vs COHR +9%; 3-yr CAGR 12% for COHR) and faster GM expansion, and notes LITE — ~50% share of AI EML lasers — is a **key merchant EML supplier to Coherent**, underlining COHR's external dependence on a competitor for a core transceiver input (Citi LITE Initiation, 2025-08-29). Bernstein ranks Coherent (incl. legacy II-VI) the **#2 global transceiver supplier in 2024 behind Innolight**, in a market increasingly dominated by Chinese players (Innolight, Eoptolink, Hisense, Accelink) — a long-run share/competition risk (Bernstein China Next Winners, 2025-10-27). InP substrate is a structural China-supply risk — the CEO reportedly traveled to China with a Trump delegation seeking InP (briefing, 2026-06-15). Stock fell ~11% on 2026-06-10 on CPO-delay concerns (JPM framed as overblown). Valuation: post-NVDA the space trades ~25x CY28 EPS for COHR/LITE — well off its historical teens (MS, 2026-05-07).

Hold both: this is the cleanest example on the page of a bull thesis and a balance-sheet question landing from the same source on the same day** (Wolfe Research "Q2 earnings-season takeaways call", 2026-08-14).
  - **🔴 THE BAN RANKING, EXPLICIT AND ORDINAL: *"Our probably best place here are certainly **Coherent number one**, I think **Lumentum number two**, **AOI number three**."*** [[AAOI]] is third for a *constraint* reason rather than an exposure one — *"they're already ramping massively. It's a little hard for us to see how much incremental they could bring on just to address the Chinese capacity."* **Wolfe adds Fabrinet as another name to look at.** ✅ **This is now the THIRD house on the same rank order (MS 08-04 named COHR the clearest beneficiary; Jefferies 08-07 *"bullish for COHR, less so LITE"*) — and it is the first to state a full ordinal ranking including AAOI, with a stated reason for the ordering.**
  - **✅ COHR MANAGEMENT'S OWN VIEW ON THE SECURITY QUESTION, from Wolfe's post-call conversation, and it is a company-sourced confirmation this page did not have: *"they confirmed for us that transceivers are a national security threat or potential security threat. **Anytime that you've got a DSP chip in an electronics product, you've got potential for a security threat.**"* They also pushed the supply-chain framing: *"does the US government really want to have only Chinese supply, or considerable Chinese supply, into US hyperscalers? Does it make more sense to have more US supply or non-Chinese supply?"*** ➤ **Note what this upgrades: the page's *Policy / China* row already reads ✓ confirms on the basis that restriction chatter *"has spurred some new customer demand and supply discussions"* (08-12 call). Management privately CONFIRMING the threat exists — with a mechanism (the DSP) rather than an assertion — is a step beyond commercial activity, and Wolfe's own conclusion is *"the fact that Lumentum is still being engaged by the US government, the fact that Coherent guys are saying there is a potential threat… it is, I think, indeed real."* ⚠️ Still a broker's account of a private conversation, not a disclosure.**
  - **🆕 CHRONOLOGY CORRECTION, worth logging because this page dates the theme to the Reuters/FCC reports of 08-04: *"this did not just start with the Reuters article of a week or two weeks ago. This started with the **Department of Defense looking for a report on the national security concerns surrounding Chinese transceivers deployed in data centers**… It's about a page long, it's an easy read, **search for the term transceiver**. But the point in all this is **this has been brewing for some time**"*** (Wolfe Research "Q2 earnings-season takeaways call", 2026-08-14). ➤ **The practical consequence: the ban theme is a POLICY PROCESS with a documented origin, not a headline — which raises the odds it survives news-cycle decay, and gives a primary document to pull. ⚠️ Wolfe references a link in its deck; the DoD document itself is NOT on disk and has not been read.**
  - **⚠️🔴 AND THE BEAR FROM THE SAME CALL — A POTENTIAL EQUITY RAISE, DERIVED FROM WOLFE'S OWN WORKING-CAPITAL MODELLING RATHER THAN FROM ANY COMPANY STATEMENT: *"I found situations like a Coherent where, if you grow the business enough, you make the right assumptions in terms of working capital, you start to look at Coherent's balance sheet and say, well, **maybe these guys need to raise some more capital**"*** (Wolfe Research "Q2 earnings-season takeaways call", 2026-08-14). ⚠️ **LABEL IT PRECISELY: this is a MODELLING INFERENCE. There is no announced transaction, no company commentary, and Wolfe supplies no leverage, cash, receivables-days or capex figures for COHR to support it. It sits inside a general theme — Wolfe says it kept *"going through some gymnastics around the balance sheet and cash flow statements… dialing in assumptions around working capital in order to get these companies to show positive cash metrics,"* and names [[SMCI]], [[AAOI]] and Celestica as companies that have ALREADY done capital transactions.** ➤ **Why it belongs on this page anyway, and under Bear: (i) it is the first source here to frame COHR's growth as a CASH problem rather than a yield or margin problem — a genuinely different axis from the 08-13/08-17 yield-disclosure bear; (ii) it interacts with the LTA structure the page treats as a pure positive, since LTAs that bundle *customer upfront capex* (Anderson, 2026-05-06) are precisely the mitigant Wolfe credits [[GLW]] with and does NOT credit COHR with; and (iii) it sets up the sharpest contrast in the whole call — Wolfe's TOP PICK on the ban theme is simultaneously the name it thinks may need equity, while [[LITE]] *"[isn't] looking for money"* because NVIDIA's $2bn funds its expansion. Ranking and balance sheet point opposite ways.** ➤ **The test is disclosure-based and near-dated: FQ1 FY27 receivables/inventory days and any shelf or convertible activity. Nothing on this page currently contradicts Wolfe's inference — and nothing supports it either.**

The existence and SHAPE of the work is recorded; nothing is quantified, and nothing should be inferred. Pull the primary before using it.**

- **📚 ARCHIVE — **DAMNANG (Substack)** · ⚠️ **independent author, NOT a broker** (Korean semis Substack, launched 2026-03-15, ~9k subscribers, paid tier; no rating, no PT, no estimates). ℹ️ **Same author already cited on this page as *Damnang / "The Optical Edge"* in the 08-04 Full-log row — DAMNANG / Damnang2 / "The Optical Edge" are ONE identity across the wiki.** 3-5 months old, historical context only; nothing below supersedes any current number on this page.**
  - **The standard-setting asymmetry inside the COHR/[[LITE]] pair, which this page had not carried: COHR is a FOUNDING MEMBER OF TWO of the three optical MSAs launched on 2026-03-12 — **Open CPX** (with [[CIEN]], [[MRVL]], Molex, Samtec and TeraHop) and **XPO** ([[ANET]]-led) — while LITE founded NONE and [[CSCO]] is absent from all three (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "The War of Light Has Begun", **2026-03-16**; and "The War of Light, Part 2: Who’s at the Table, Who’s Not, and Who’s Hiding", **2026-03-17**). ➤ **The strategic reading is the useful part and it is two-edged: Open CPX standardises the SOCKET, thermals and pinout for the optical engine, i.e. it is *"an attempt to build an open optical engine ecosystem OUTSIDE the fully vertically integrated CPO model"* — *"if Open CPX succeeds, optical engines become COMMODITY components. If it fails, the vertically integrated players dominate."* [[AVGO]] and [[NVDA]] are absent from Open CPX for exactly that reason. COHR is therefore betting that a MULTI-VENDOR engine market is bigger for it than a proprietary one — which is precisely the bet PhotonLink productises.**
  - **The breadth claim, stated as an OFC-2026 scoreboard rather than a slogan: COHR was *"the only company at OFC 2026 to simultaneously demonstrate 1.6T transceivers using FOUR different technologies: SiPh, InP CW, InP EML, and GaAs VCSEL,"* plus compatibility with **three different DSP vendor solutions**, three 6-inch InP fabs (Järfälla / Sherman / Zürich), a **400mW CW laser**, **200G/400G photodiodes**, a **400G Differential EML** and a **400G silicon PIC MZM** for 3.2T (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "The War of Light, A Laser Shortage", **2026-03-31**). Same piece: the VCSEL franchise is *"close to a duopoly between Coherent and Broadcom,"* built on the **iPhone Face ID** volume base — *"hundreds of millions of VCSELs per year to Apple is how Coherent built its economies of scale."* Marks the **NVDA $2B investment at $257/share** (vs LITE at $695). ➤ **This is the independent, pre-print basis for the "portfolio breadth is the product" argument JPM heard from management five months later — and it is the same argument PhotonLink monetises.**
  - **The multi-wavelength-array gap, which is the counterweight to the breadth claim: COHR *"demonstrated an ELSFP but standalone ARRAY DIE productization remains limited,"* with an **8-laser ELSFP** unveiled and a **400mW CW at ECOC 2025** — against a field of independents (Sivers, Scintil, Ayar, Xscape, Ranovus) whose pitch to hyperscalers is precisely that COHR/LITE capacity is read as NVDA-linked (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "Sivers Semiconductors: Verifying the Thesis After a 2,100% Run in Six Months", **2026-05-27**). ⚠️ **Five months stale and no production data either way — logged as the shape of the competitive risk in WDM-based second-generation CPO, not as evidence about it.**
  - **Historical marks retained for the audit trail (as at 2026-04-30): Q2 FY26 was the **fifth consecutive beat**; **datacenter book-to-bill above 4x** *"providing demand visibility through 2027"*; **6-inch InP wafer capacity at 80% of target**; Q3 FY26 guide **$1.7-1.84B**; **S&P 500 inclusion on 2026-03-23**; the **Munich business-unit divestiture** framed as margin-accretive; and an OFC lineup of **6.4T socketed SiPh CPO + ELS, multimode VCSEL CPO, 400G InP modulator on silicon, and multi-rail optical transport (4 C+L band rails in 1RU)** (**DAMNANG (Substack)** — "Optical Investment Map Stock Review: Who Moved and Who’s Next?", **2026-05-01**). ⚠️ **Superseded on every current line by the FQ4 FY26 print and the 6-inch disclosures above. The one item worth keeping live is the >4x datacenter book-to-bill — it is the earliest dated statement on this page of the "FY27 basically completely booked out" claim management made four months later.**

## Catalysts / what to watch
- **🆕 2026-09-21 — PHOTONLINK LAUNCH: establish the PLATFORM, LANE RATE AND MODULATION FORMAT, not just the positioning.** The FQ1 FY27 synthesis lists PhotonLink as a genuinely net-new BULL item (platform-level content rather than components, first revenue in the December quarter). 🔴 **An independent engineer has asked whether it is COHR's VCSEL CPO/NPO brand and argues that "200G PAM4 on a VCSEL will not work from a reliability perspective… going to FAIL GR-468"** (Irrational Analysis, 2026-08-13). ➤ **If PhotonLink is VCSEL-based at 200G PAM4, GR-468 qualification becomes a dated, falsifiable test of the page's newest bull item. If it is not VCSEL-based, the objection lapses. Either way the launch should settle it.**
- **🆕 A CPO/EML LASER-YIELD DISCLOSURE — the standing ask, now with a specific form.** COHR has published **no** CPO-laser yield data, while [[LITE]] has published drive-current spread, power-conversion efficiency, RIN and linewidth at a stated temperature. ⚠️ **Until COHR publishes the equivalent, management's "6-inch is cost structure, not a yield gap" (08-12) and the "their CPO laser yield is bad" inference (Irrational Analysis, 08-13) are symmetric assertions and the page cannot close the margin debate. The specific ask: yield for EML at a stated DATA RATE, and yield for CW laser at a stated POWER CLASS.**
- **🆕 FIRST AXT 6-INCH InP VOLUME — watch it as a COHR variable, not only an [[AXTI]] one.** The exculpatory version of the yield bear is that **6-inch InP wafer UNIFORMITY at the substrate supplier (Sumitomo, hypothesised) is the constraint, and AXT does not yet ship 6-inch (still R&D)** (Irrational Analysis, 2026-08-13). ➤ **COHR's own 2026-06-26 AXT agreement ($22.3m prepay) is specifically for the development and supply of 6-INCH wafers — so first AXT 6-inch volume is a direct test of whether COHR's 6-inch economics improve for reasons that have nothing to do with its own fab.**
- **Q4 FY26 print (≈Aug 2026):** FY26 close + first FY27 framing; watch GM toward >42% target and capex trajectory.
- **6-inch InP milestone:** internal capacity doubling expected next (June) quarter — a quarter early; Zurich third site starts early CY27.
- **CPO revenue inflection:** initial scale-out H2 CY26, scale-up H2 CY27 (NVIDIA lead customer + others); watch NVIDIA GTC, where JPM expects scale-up CPO (Lumentum + Coherent) to feature heavily (JPM Hardware & Networking, 2025-12-15).
- **New LTAs:** additional multi-customer LTAs expected to close near-term, including hyperscalers and system OEMs (Anderson, 2026-05-06).
- **New vectors:** Multi-Rail revenue H1 CY27; thermal (Thermadite/TEG) H2 CY27; semicap industrial recovery through CY26.
- **JPM investor meetings (NYC, 2026-06-17):** post-selloff management access (Anderson + IR Silverstein) — read-through to sentiment.
- **🆕 FQ1 FY27 WORKING-CAPITAL DISCLOSURE — the test of a new bear that no company statement supports. Wolfe's balance-sheet modelling concludes *"maybe these guys need to raise some more capital"* (Wolfe, 2026-08-14 — an INFERENCE, not an announced transaction).** ➤ **What to check, in order: receivables and inventory days against the >$3bn-quarter trajectory; whether the LTAs' *customer upfront capex* component (Anderson, 2026-05-06) is actually funding capacity the way [[GLW]]'s customer prepayments do; and any shelf, convertible or ATM activity. ⚠️ Wolfe supplied NO COHR-specific balance-sheet numbers — this is a watch item precisely because the claim is currently untestable from the page.**
- **🆕 OBTAIN WOLFE'S *"TRANSCEIVER DREAMING"* NOTE (Coolout newsletter, weekend of 2026-08-08/09) — it contains the house's 2028/29 transceiver-revenue math BOTH with and without a Chinese transceiver ban, and the call described the ban-case numbers only as *"absolutely enormous"* with caveats.** ⚠️ **Not on disk; no figures were stated on the call. This is the single largest un-ingested sizing artefact touching the ban theme on this page.**

## Risks
- **🔴 NO CPO-LASER YIELD DISCLOSURE — an information risk, and the one the bears are now exploiting explicitly.** COHR has published no yield data for CPO/CW lasers at a stated power class, nor for EMLs at a stated data rate, while [[LITE]] has published drive-current spread, power-conversion efficiency, RIN and linewidth at a specified temperature. ⚠️ **The consequence is structural rather than rhetorical: the margin debate cannot be settled from the outside, so an engineering-grade inference ("the only way COHR's numbers make sense is if CPO-laser yield is bad" — Irrational Analysis, 2026-08-13) sits at parity with management's own framing ("the 6-inch benefit is cost structure, 4x output at half the cost, NOT a yield gap" — 08-12 call). An MS analyst put the same question on the call. The absence of data is itself the risk, and it will recur every quarter the guide midpoint disappoints.**
- **⚠️ 6-INCH InP WAFER UNIFORMITY — a SUPPLIER-side version of the yield risk, and partly outside COHR's control.** The hypothesis is that 6-inch InP *should* yield better (new-generation Aixtron MOCVD reactors, semi-modern ASML litho), so weak yield may trace to **wafer uniformity from the substrate supplier** rather than to COHR's fab, with **[[AXTI]] not yet shipping 6-inch (still under R&D)** leaving the incumbent (Sumitomo, hypothesised) effectively sole-source at that diameter (Irrational Analysis, 2026-08-13). ⚠️ **Unverified and explicitly labelled a conspiracy theory by its own author. Carried because it is testable and because COHR's 2026-06-26 AXT prepayment is specifically for 6-inch development and supply — i.e. COHR is already funding the second source this risk implies it needs.**
- **⚠️ VCSEL AT 200G PAM4 — a reliability objection aimed at the newest bull item: "200G PAM4 on a VCSEL will not work from a reliability perspective… going to fail GR-468," and even if it passed, energy efficiency suffers on driver complexity, FEC for crosstalk and Rx TIA sensitivity burden** (Irrational Analysis, 2026-08-13). ⚠️ **CONDITIONAL on PhotonLink actually being a VCSEL CPO/NPO platform, which is the author's QUESTION rather than an established fact — the 09-21 launch should resolve it. The same source concedes COHR "has great VCSELs, second only to Broadcom," so the objection is about the DATA RATE, not the franchise.**
- **⚠️ EML→SiPho CONTENT DILUTION — milder for COHR than for [[LITE]], but not zero: "a SiPho transceiver has 50% REDUCED InP content versus an EML based transceiver while having significantly higher ACTIVE ALIGNMENT content… It is TRIVIAL to pivot manufacturing from EML based to SiPho based"** (Irrational Analysis, 2026-08-04). ⚠️ **COHR's hybrid InP+SiPh model is the version JPM calls structurally defensive against InP overbuild (2025-12-15), so this is a smaller hit than on a pure-InP merchant model. Two live edges remain: it erodes the InP-vertical-integration premium at the margin, and the value migrates into active alignment — where the same author says Chinese module makers are an order of magnitude better than the West. COHR uses Tower ([[TSEM]]) for the SiPho PIC, so a pivot also moves content to a foundry.**
- **InP / materials supply (10-K, 2025-08-15):** dependence on rare-earth minerals, noble gases and InP substrates; China controls majority of InP output — geopolitical/export-control exposure to a core input. Note ongoing reliance on merchant EML supply from Lumentum (~50% AI EML share) for part of the transceiver portfolio (Citi, 2025-08-29).
- **Execution on capacity ramp:** 6-inch InP yield/qualification risk; OCS production bottlenecks (one cleared Q3, others possible); competitor (Lumentum) seen as further ahead on capacity control.
- **SiPh substitution:** rising silicon-photonics adoption favors cheaper, smaller CW lasers over higher-ASP EMLs — a market-level revenue headwind (sharper for pure-InP merchant models than for COHR's hybrid model, but a watch item) (JPM Hardware & Networking, 2025-12-15).
- **CPO timing & competitive position:** scale-up CPO is H2 CY27 and unproven at volume; copper-vs-optics penetration debate (10–50% by 2030 per Corning internal range); peers question liquid-crystal OCS durability.
- **Competition / share:** Chinese transceiver vendors dominate the global market and COHR ranks #2 behind Innolight (2024) — long-run pricing/share pressure (Bernstein China Next Winners, 2025-10-27).
- **Valuation / multiple:** re-rated to ~25x CY28; any demand-digestion or supply over-build undercuts the equity story.
- **⚠️🔴 WORKING-CAPITAL / POTENTIAL EQUITY-RAISE RISK — new axis, and it is a MODELLING INFERENCE with no company statement behind it: *"I found situations like a Coherent where, if you grow the business enough, you make the right assumptions in terms of working capital, you start to look at Coherent's balance sheet and say, well, maybe these guys need to raise some more capital"*** (Wolfe Research "Q2 earnings-season takeaways call", 2026-08-14). ⚠️ **No announced transaction, no COHR-specific balance-sheet figures supplied, and it sits inside a sector-wide theme in which [[SMCI]], [[AAOI]] and Celestica have ALREADY raised. Logged because it is a genuinely DIFFERENT risk from everything else in this list — the others are yield, margin, timing and multiple risks; this one is a CASH risk — and because the same source explicitly exempts [[GLW]] (customer prepayments) and [[LITE]] (NVIDIA's $2bn), which makes the exemption of COHR pointed rather than incidental.** ➤ **Test at FQ1 FY27: receivables/inventory days, and whether the LTAs' upfront-customer-capex component behaves like GLW's customer funding.**
- **Customer concentration / China end-market:** hyperscaler-concentrated demand; export-control and complex regulatory exposure across a multi-country manufacturing footprint (10-K).

## In the inbox / theme read-through
- **JPM "Watts Up! Shift to 800V" (2026-06-09):** Coherent named among Wolfspeed's **SiC power-semiconductor peers** (with Infineon, Mitsubishi, Navitas, onsemi, Power Integrations, ROHM, STMicro) in the 800V wide-bandgap power-device opportunity (WBG market $1bn→$5bn+ by 2030, 30%+ CAGR). A peripheral, optionality-only mention — COHR's equity story remains optical interconnect, not 800V power semis — but flags a SiC-materials adjacency worth tracking (JPM "800V", 2026-06-09).
- **SIG/Hosseini "Tech Talk" — Tower Semiconductor fireside chat (2026-07-01):** in a supply-chain read-through unrelated to COHR's own reporting, Tower Semiconductor (TSEM) management named **Coherent as its partner on an "all-silicon" route to 400G/lane** (Tower SiPho platform + Coherent's design) — one of two approaches TSEM is pursuing to reach the next SerDes-lane speed, alongside a heterogeneous-InP route via OpenLight. Thin, foundry-side confirmation that COHR's silicon-photonics IP is being designed into a third-party (non-LITE) heterogeneous-integration roadmap; no COHR-specific financial detail (SIG "Tech Talk: Inside the Transceiver Manufacturing Supply Chain," Mehdi Hosseini, 2026-07-01). See [optical-cpo theme](themes/optical-cpo.md) for the full TSEM readout.
- **🆕 ⚠️ BACKFILL, AND IT IS SEVEN MONTHS OLD — READ IT AS A JANUARY REFERENCE MARK, NOT AS NEWS: SemiAnalysis's "CPO Book" (Dylan Patel, Daniel Nishball, Myron Xie et al, published 2026-01-01) puts COHR as the SECOND source into NVIDIA's CPO switch, behind [[LITE]].** Verbatim: *"Key industry players capable of producing CW laser units include **Broadcom (U.S.), Furukawa (Japan), Lumentum (U.S.), Coherent (U.S.), Yuanjie (China), and Shijia (China)**. Lumentum, Coherent, Furukawa and Broadcom usually **charge higher prices** than Chinese vendors… **We expect Lumentum to be the SOLE supplier for Nvidia's INITIAL batches of CPO switch shipments, and COHERENT MAY ENTER AS THE SECOND SUPPLIER IN LATE 2026.** Chinese manufacturers could see opportunities down the road, as CW laser sources are considered **relatively standardized and commoditized in general, but there is still somewhat of a moat around building the HIGH-POWER laser sources that are required for CPO applications."* (SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel et al, "CPO Book", 2026-01-01)
  - ★ **THE REASON TO CARRY A STALE FORECAST: IT HAS SINCE BEEN SCORED, AND IT WAS RIGHT.** Management told the 08-12 call that the **ultra-high-power CW laser for CPO — including the NVIDIA partnership — is in production wafers now with revenue in the DECEMBER quarter (fiscal Q2)** (COHR Q4 FY26 earnings call, 2026-08-13). **A January third-party supply-chain map that called COHR "second supplier, late 2026" landed on the calendar quarter, from seven months out.** ➜ **Two consequences. (1) It independently corroborates the second-source framing this page carries from PhotonCap (07-29, "second sourcing is in progress, not a hypothesis") and from the BTG expert call (06-25, "only COHR and LITE a reliable UHP laser source") — three unconnected sources, one conclusion. (2) It bounds the bull: SemiAnalysis has COHR as the SECOND entrant behind LITE on NVIDIA's initial batches, which is a share statement, not a shut-out — and it explicitly calls the CW-laser category "relatively standardized and commoditized" with the moat sitting ONLY in the high-power tier. That is the same knife-edge as the NPO-content-parity dispute: COHR's economics depend on the socket staying high-power.**
  - **The system arithmetic behind it, useful for sizing COHR content per switch and net-new to this page:** the **NVIDIA X800-Q3450 CPO switch (115.2T, scale-out)** uses **18 ELS modules, each containing 8 CW DFB laser chips** (⇒ **144 CW DFB chips per switch**), each chip *"delivering ~**350mW** of power"*; the initial version runs **72 optical engines at 1.6Tbit/s**, later versions likely **36 OEs at 3.2Tbit/s at ~$1,000 per unit including FAUs**, for an **optical-engine BOM of ~$35–40k per switch**. Each 1.6T OE carries an **FAU with 20 fibres (8 Tx, 8 Rx, 4 for the external laser)**, **1,440 fibres per system**. ⚠️ **Cross-check against COHR's own product marks: Citi (07-16) has COHR showing a 400mW InP CW laser at OFC 2026 — comfortably above the ~350mW spec SemiAnalysis attributes to the NVIDIA design, which supports rather than contradicts qualification.** ⚠️ **But note the omission, and it is a real one: SemiAnalysis names TFC Optical, Senko and FOCI as the FAU leaders (plus Sumitomo and AFR), and does NOT name Coherent — while MS's scale-up primer (07-13) credits COHR with FAUs and polarization-maintaining fibre in its stack. Either COHR's FAU position is below the threshold SemiAnalysis was mapping, or the MS stack overstates it. Unresolved; flagged rather than reconciled.** (SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel et al, "CPO Book", 2026-01-01) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/newslettersemianalysiscom_api_v1_post_pdf_postId178153689.html)

**FQ1 FY27 synthesis (opened 2026-08-12 · re-scored 2026-08-13 off the primary transcript):** the print and the call resolved the *delivery* question decisively — revenue, EPS, backlog, the InP ramp and the >$3B-quarter target all landed at or above every published bar, and the +80% y/y laser-output disclosure converts the September quarter from a forecast into arithmetic. What the call did **not** resolve is the two things the multiple is actually paid for. **(1) Margin: the guide midpoint is 40.5%, not the 41.5% the page had carried and not the 41–41.5% the buy side wanted — so the "COHR is the margin catalyst" trade MS previewed did not pay, and the >42% target now has to be BEATEN rather than met to re-rate the name. (2) NPO-vs-CPO content parity is asserted by both issuers and denied by the analyst closest to the architecture, with zero evidence on either side.** The genuinely net-new bull items are **PhotonLink (09-21 launch, platform-level content rather than components, first revenue in the December quarter)**, the **OCS TAM history ($2B → >$4B, "we may have even been conservative")**, and the **policy chatter already producing customer manufacturing conversations**. The genuinely net-new bear is **Nutty's shrinking-buyer-count argument** — that as conversion moves inside the rack, COHR's products become captive parts for a handful of system designers and the receive-side DSP migrates out of its domain entirely, which is the mirror image of the NVDA-stake bull this page has treated as unambiguously positive. **➜ The trade this page has tracked since 08-07 — "COHR outperforms LITE if and only if it shows 6-inch supply" — should be scored a PARTIAL WIN on the operating evidence (a quarter-early double, 4x output at half cost, a mechanical >80% growth floor) and a LOSS on the tape (−5% vs LITE flat).**

**🆕 FQ1 FY27 synthesis — 08-17 update (the third-party engineering pass). The synthesis above is unchanged and stands; this appends what the newest sources changed, and it is about EVIDENCE rather than direction.** The two unresolved items were margin and NPO-vs-CPO content parity. **On sequencing — parity's weaker cousin — the page now has independent confirmation: Fabrinet, the layer that physically builds both, says NPO is "probably… a more near-term opportunity than CPO" and that it is working on CPO "with a handful of customers… not yet at full-scale volumes." That corroborates management's "almost every customer" framing without touching content parity, which still has zero evidence on either side.** On margin, the newest input is the opposite of corroboration: **an independent engineer infers from the GM gap versus [[LITE]] that COHR's CPO-laser yield is bad, and the reason the claim cannot be dismissed is that COHR publishes no yield data while LITE publishes plots — an MS analyst asked the same question on the call and did not get one either.** ➤ **The correct scoring is uncomfortable and should stay uncomfortable: the bear contradicts nothing COHR has disclosed BECAUSE COHR HAS DISCLOSED NOTHING on this axis, so management's "cost structure, not a yield gap" and the engineer's "yield is bad" sit at evidentiary parity. Against it, and kept deliberately, an analyst on Nokia's call ranks COHR's InP ramp AHEAD of LITE's — a different metric, pointing the other way.** Two dated tests now exist where before there were only views: **the 09-21 PhotonLink launch (establish platform, lane rate and modulation — the GR-468 objection applies only if it is VCSEL at 200G PAM4)** and **first AXT 6-inch volume, which would test whether COHR's 6-inch economics improve for supplier reasons rather than fab reasons.** ➤ **Net: no number on this page moved, and the page got materially more testable.**

**🆕 FQ1 FY27 synthesis — 08-25 update (the JPM feedback-call pass). The two syntheses above stand; this changes NOTHING in the forecast and adds two things the page did not have: a QUARTERLY BRIDGE and a POSITIONING read.** The bridge is JPM’s own modelling cadence to the >$3bn quarter — **+10% q/q through FQ3, ~+8% in FQ4, GM +20bps per quarter, opex $440m/$460m/$490m, shares +3m per quarter** — which is the first time this page can see what the ">$3bn exit" actually requires line by line, and the answer is that the OPEX slope, not the gross margin, is what governs whether the operating leverage the bulls assume shows up. The positioning read is that **the buy-side did not defend the name**: *"it just feels like people want to short this name and we didn’t see any long only defending this… in the market,"* with the complaint concentrated on **margin disclosure** rather than on any delivered number. ➤ **Net: the two standing debates (6-inch cost curve, CPO/NPO content) are untouched, and a THIRD has been made concrete — the product-line decomposition. 800G grows through 2027, 1.6T is "early but shipping," 400G-and-below is DECLINING, and management would not date the crossover. Since the crossover is what sets FY27 transceiver ASP, and the 50/50 → 75% 6-inch mix path is what sets its cost, the single disclosure that would end the "vitriol" is a transceiver gross-margin bridge. That is now the most valuable thing COHR could say at ECOC or on the FQ1 call, and it is entirely within management’s control.**

However, here it’s a bit nuanced in that they’re focused more on **CONSUMING A LOT OF THE CAPACITY INTERNALLY** as they push their own **AMPS AND LINE CARDS and COMPETE WITH THE NETWORKING OEMs MORE DIRECTLY** — and they’re seeing good success in doing so just given their vertical integration."* Against LITE, whose LTAs cover *"most of their portfolio EXCLUDING transceivers"* and who sells pumps merchant.** ➤ **Same component, two opposite strategies: LITE sells the pump, COHR eats it and sells the line card. It just feels like PEOPLE WANT TO SHORT THIS NAME and we DIDN’T SEE ANY LONG ONLY DEFENDING this name in the market."*** ➤ **Independent of the fundamentals, that is a bidless-tape observation from the desk that hosted the call, and it explains the −5% better than "Industrial was soft." ⚠️ Note the same host reports the OPPOSITE for LITE on the same call.
