As the electrical-optical conversion moves from beyond the rack to inside it, the products these companies make turn from independent components serving many customers into CAPTIVE PARTS for a small number of switch and system designers. What is more, optics' clean signal transmission ends up REMOVING one of the important pieces of IP, the receive-side DSP, and that function migrates into the SerDes (which is not these companies' domain). NO CUSTOMER ON EARTH PAYS FOR A FUNCTION THAT IS GONE. The $2 billion Nvidia invested in each of Lumentum and Coherent is likewise ANOTHER NAME FOR THESE COMPANIES' BUYER DEPENDENCE."** Second dependency, also net-new here: **"an optical integration platform has no choice but to rely on a system like TSMC's COUPE. Otherwise there is no 'integration' at all."** ➜ **"Investing in the three optics stocks is NOT a bet that 'light wins.' Demand for 800G has been the primary growth driver thus far but WE EXPECT THE 1.6T INFLECTION IS NOW UNDERWAY OFFERING A MEANINGFUL ASP UPLIFT (~2X). Broadly speaking, CONCERNS ABOUT THE IMPACT OF CPO ON TRANSCEIVER DEMAND HAVE ACQUIESCED TO INVESTOR EXCITEMENT ON THE OPPORTUNITY FOR LASER TAM EXPANSION TIED TO NPO ROLLOUTS."** ⚠️🔴 **ON SUPPLY — THIS IS THE ACTIONABLE WARNING AND IT IS DATED: "we would highlight CAPACITY EXPANSION AS PERHAPS THE SINGLE GREATEST AREA OF CONCERN. THE RUBBER SEEMS TO MEET THE ROAD IN C28 WHERE A CONSIDERABLE AMOUNT OF CAPACITY COMES ONLINE INTENDED TO MEET DEMAND FOR SCALE-UP NPO/CPO, WHERE ROADMAPS HAVE YET TO BE FINALIZED. IF THESE RAMPS SLIP, THE INDUSTRY RISKS A CLASSIC CAPACITY OVERSHOOT INTO C28 AND THE PRICING GAINS THAT DROVE MARGIN EXPANSION WOULD BE THE FIRST CASUALTY."** **Note the precise structure of the risk: capacity is being built for a scale-up architecture WHOSE ROADMAP IS NOT YET FIXED. It is not a demand call — it is a timing-mismatch call, and the loss function is margin, not revenue.** 🔴 **ON SUBSTRATES — a fresh intra-quarter tightening, and it names the chokepoint: "LITE MANAGEMENT HIGHLIGHTED INCREMENTAL TIGHTNESS DEVELOPING INTRA-QUARTER RELATING TO INCREASED DEMAND FROM NPO. Outside of a few Japanese vendors (SUMITOMO ELECTRIC, JX), SUPPLY FOR InP CRYSTAL WAFER SUPPLY STEMS LARGELY FROM CHINA ([[AXTI]]) AND FALLS UNDER STRICT EXPORT CONTROLS. We expect this is something to watch for on BOTH earnings calls."** ⚠️ **This is the same InP bottleneck the page already carries from the supplier side (07-31), now confirmed as WORSENING intra-quarter and explicitly tied to NPO demand — and it sits under Chinese export control, i.e. the identical policy lever the US is proposing to pull on transceivers. Symmetric escalation risk.** **ON THE CHINA BAN — Curtis's read is bullish for COHR specifically and he ranks the beneficiaries: "In a market where Chinese suppliers INNOLIGHT, EOPTOLINK, and others hold DOMINANT GLOBAL SHARE (~60%+), the news reads DIRECTIONALLY BULLISH FOR COHERENT AND TO A LESSER EXTENT LUMENTUM, as hyperscalers would be forced to SHIFT QUALIFICATION AND VOLUME TOWARD WESTERN MODULE SUPPLIERS. For merchant silicon suppliers ([[MRVL]]/[[AVGO]]) we see the read as BROADLY NEUTRAL over the long-term, since DSP shipments into Chinese module mak[ers]…"** **VALUATION: "LITE/COHR enter earnings both trading at 22x THEIR RESPECTIVE C28 EPS OF $37/$15, UP 130% AND 75% YTD."** ⚠️ **Same multiple, same year, very different EPS bases — worth carrying because it means the two names are being priced identically on C28 despite different mix and different CPO/NPO leverage.** **Diary: JPM post-earnings COHR/LITE analyst callback Fri 08-14 10am ET (JPM · Joshua Meyers, 08-05); COHR + LITE both presenting at JPM's HW & Semis Management Access Forum, Palo Alto, 08-12, alongside optical panels (Ranovus, Avicena) and the 650 Group.** (Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-05; JPM · Joshua Meyers, 2026-08-05) |

(2) The InP/EML/CW-laser share trajectory (16% → 27.6% by 2028) is the number to watch, because it is the same chokepoint LITE flagged as tightening intra-quarter. **Quarter synthesis:** the flow is broadly confirmatory of the management thesis — 6" InP as the differentiator, CPO/UHP lasers as the COHR/LITE moat (corroborated by BTG). **06-28: FundaAI explicitly validates the moat durability** — Chinese capacity expansion is real but confined to commodity transceivers; the UHP laser + InP substrate track is too fast-moving for mature-node catch-up. The only friction is **valuation, not operational**: BofA endorses the fundamentals but stays at NEUTRAL on the multiple (41x CY27). **07-13: MS's Updated Scale-Up Primer adds a structural bull mark** — COHR named a "clearest beneficiary" of optical into scale-up (with LITE/GLW), model rebuilt for CPO % (2028E rev nudged to $12.86bn), mgmt "the most aggressive on scale-up sizing (orders of magnitude larger than scale-out)"; MS's ~$70bn-by-2030 scale-up TAM and "sell-offs overdone" call cut against the CPO-timing bear, while pushing meaningful scale-up CPO out to Feynman (small 2028, stronger 2029+) — the same later-adoption slope Irrational Analysis's 07-11 dissent debates on laser-quality grounds. **07-14 adds two:** Rothschild/Redburn's AI-monetisation strategy screen names **COHR a North American top Buy** (optical-interconnect leader levered to AI DC networking + NVDA $2bn, clears its FCF/RoCE/leverage gates) — a strategy-level endorsement, not a fresh initiation/PT; and a fresh **650 Group/Weckel** expert call gives a market-level read (CPO partial-2028/meaningful-2029, OCS ~$4.5–5B, DCI ~$30B/thirds, copper pushed longer, 3.2T not material till 2029) that sits slightly more measured than management but on the same CPO-timing slope, and is supportive of pluggables persisting near-term. **07-16: Citi's new InP supply model** reinforces the supply-gated bull — laser-chip supply below demand through 2028 (EML gap 33%/20%/11%, CW gap widening to 55% by 2028), with COHR the 6-inch leader and most-internal supplier; Buy/PT $420 reiterated, pullback a buying opportunity — the residual COHR-specific swing being whether Broadcom buys CPO components from COHR or self-integrates.

**🆕 Quarter synthesis — 08-20 addendum (the Wolfe pass). Direction unchanged; what is new is a SECOND, previously unrepresented bear axis and a company-sourced upgrade to the policy leg.** Wolfe's 08-14 takeaways call gives this page (i) the **first full ordinal ranking on the transceiver-ban theme — COHR #1, [[LITE]] #2, [[AAOI]] #3** (AAOI third because it is already supply-committed), with Fabrinet named as an additional play, making Wolfe the **third house** on the COHR-first order; (ii) **COHR management's own private confirmation that transceivers are a potential national-security threat, with a mechanism — *"anytime that you've got a DSP chip in an electronics product, you've got potential for a security threat"*** — plus the US-vs-Chinese-supply framing; and (iii) **a chronology correction: the theme originates in a DEPARTMENT OF DEFENSE request for a report on Chinese transceivers in data centres, not in the 08-04 Reuters/FCC reports** — *"this has been brewing for some time,"* which materially lowers the odds it decays with the news cycle. ⚠️ **Against all of that, the same call opens a bear this page has never carried: from Wolfe's own working-capital modelling, *"maybe these guys need to raise some more capital."* It is an INFERENCE with no announced transaction, no company commentary and no COHR-specific balance-sheet figures — but it is a CASH axis, orthogonal to the yield/margin/timing/multiple debates that dominate this page, and it is pointed rather than incidental because the same source EXEMPTS [[GLW]] by name (customers funding capacity) and [[LITE]] by name (NVIDIA's $2bn) while exempting COHR from nothing, including COHR's own upfront-customer-capex LTA structure.** ➤ **Net: the ban leg strengthens and gains a documented policy origin; the balance sheet becomes an open question with a named source and no evidence either way. Both are testable at FQ1 FY27 — the ban on scope/grandfathering, the cash on receivables and inventory days.** ⚠️ **One un-ingested artefact is now known to exist and matters: Wolfe's *"Transceiver Dreaming"* note (Coolout, prior weekend) carries the house's 2028/29 transceiver math with and without a ban, described only as *"absolutely enormous."* No figure was stated — do not infer one.**

  🔴 **THE HEADLINE, AND THE FRAMING MATTERS MORE THAN THE CLAIM: Irrational Analysis (2026-08-13 — independent Substack by a working engineer, self-declared long semis) argues the transceiver-mix explanation does not account for COHR's gross-margin gap versus [[LITE]] and that "the only way COHR's numbers make sense is if CPO-LASER YIELD IS BAD." The reason it is logged rather than dismissed is that COHR DISCLOSES NO CPO-LASER YIELD DATA — no EML yield at a stated data rate, no CW-laser yield at a stated power class — while LITE publishes drive-current spread, power-conversion efficiency, RIN and linewidth at a specified temperature. An MS analyst asked the leading version on the call ("is your miniscule gross margin expansion because of mix shift or 6in yield?") and did not get one either. ➤ SO: THE NOTE CONTRADICTS NOTHING THE COMPANY HAS DISCLOSED, BECAUSE THE COMPANY HAS DISCLOSED NOTHING MEASURABLE ON THIS AXIS — which puts management's "the 6-inch benefit is cost structure, 4x output at half the cost, NOT a yield gap" (08-12 call) and the engineer's inference at EVIDENTIARY PARITY. That asymmetry IS the finding, and it is a real disclosure gap at a company whose bull case rests on a manufacturing advantage. It also revives, from a second source, the 2026-05-08 6-inch yield skepticism the Q3 FY26 `Sinal` table still marks unresolved.**

- **2026-07-31 (wiki-ingest) — direct supplier-side evidence on InP substrate scarcity, and Coherent is named in it.** No rating or PT superseded. **The datapoint: AXT's 2Q26 call disclosed a $25.4M long-term-agreement payment FROM COHERENT (plus $22.3M from Casela), booked as a liability and recognised as it ships — so it is NOT in AXT's reported quarterly revenue.** ⚠️ **Significant upfront cash to pre-fund a substrate vendor's capacity, rather than relying on spot allocation, is a scarcity price — and it is the most concrete evidence on this page that indium-phosphide supply is the binding constraint on the 800G→1.6T transition.** Supporting context from the same primary call: **AXT is ahead of schedule doubling InP capacity in 2026, the InP revenue opportunity is on track to MORE THAN TRIPLE by end-2026 with "continued significant expansion in 2027," backlog is >$100M, and "demand outpaces supply, no matter how fast they add capacity."** The demand mechanism maps directly onto Coherent's mix: **the 800G→1.6T transition with MORE LASERS PER TRANSCEIVER plus higher-spec 6-inch substrates, and hyperscalers "advancing toward NPO and CPO."** Also useful: **customers buying gallium arsenide are "also very interested in InP"** — VCSEL and InP demand are moving together, consistent with Coherent's dual exposure. AXT expects to be **"by far the largest indium phosphide producer in the world"** post-2027 expansion, with **China at ~40-60% of its InP revenue.** Backdrop: COHR named among JPM's beneficiaries of **~$750bn 2026 hyperscaler capex (+80% y/y)**, and sits in BofA's **second-wave** AI re-entry list of names still 30-50% off highs. **JPM COHR + LITE callback diarised 2026-08-14 10am ET; optical panel (Ranovus, Avicena) + 650 Group at the 08-12 HW & Semis forum.** Cross-filed [[AXTI]], [[LITE]], [[GLW]], [[POET]], [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md).

The same day Lumentum got its $2B, Coherent got an identical $2B. Coherent's OFC 2026 release formalizes its 6 inch InP ramp and a 400mW CW laser, and AAOI announced sampling of a 400mW narrow linewidth laser in December 2025… Competing capacity lands outside the FY27 LTA window." The named ELS trio is new here — Sumitomo had appeared only as substrate/capacity, never as a named NVDA external-light-source vendor.** The **400mW CW laser at OFC 2026** already sits on the page via Citi 07-16 — treated as independent corroboration, not a duplicate entry. **Also net-new: NVDA's high-power-CW guidance to LITE *and COHR* at ~40MM units in January rising to ~100MM by April/May, back-solving to ~200-300k CPO switches in 2027 and 600-800k in 2028, aligned to TSMC COUPE (Aurelion citing Global Semi Research, 2026-06-10) — ⚠️ channel figures, not company disclosure, per Aurelion's own weighting; and the specific SemiAnalysis 2026-06-09 mechanism behind the ~11% June-10 drawdown already on the page (scale-out CPO revised down through FY27, scale-up pushed to FY29, CPO output driven by a single very limited ASIC with difficult integration economics). PhotonCap's split of that risk is the mix-relevant addition: an integration-driven delay "pushes CW and ELSFP revenue out while extending the EML cycle," since demand "stays in pluggables longer, and pluggables are EML intensive" — only a broad AI-capex slowdown hurts both architectures.** **Competitor-management characterisation logged as such, not as independent assessment: "Lumentum and Coherent are the two companies left standing from telecom… Coherent is stronger at the systems level and is working to catch up on the component side."** Cross-filed [LITE.md](LITE.md), [themes/optical-cpo.md](themes/optical-cpo.md). |
| 2026-07-26 (/wiki-ingest — Outlook, weekend batch) | **2 intra-quarter rows. Nothing superseded.** **The 07-23 Nokia optical-DCI read-through now has its number, and it is a ~2x beat against a bogey investors were actively debating the day before: AI order intake €2.8bn vs a €1.3-1.5bn buy-side bogey** (JPM/Scott Silver) — the best-quantified AI/cloud optical order datapoint on this page, and upstream demand for precisely the transceiver/laser content COHR supplies. **The reaction is the part worth internalising: Nokia reversed from +7% at the open to roughly flat, having already fallen ~40% from its highs, with JPM writing "I struggle to identify what more the company realistically could have delivered today beyond raising guidance."** Demand confirmed, tape not paying — the same week's pattern at INTC, TSM, ASML, GOOGL and GEV. Feedback has Nokia sustaining 20-25x → €12-15/share on 2028 numbers. **Second, a supply-side item aimed at the laser moat, on a 2028 horizon: JPM raised WIN Semiconductors (OW, PT NT$336 → NT$373) 2026 revenue growth to 30% from ~10% (Optical +50%+) on earlier-than-expected optical receiver chip (PD) mass production — "late 2Q vs 2H26" — with utilisation 65% (2Q) → 70%+ (3Q26e).** The concentration detail: **only ONE PD project is in mass production, yet that single customer ("likely a major US design house") takes AI optical comm to a high-single-digit mix of WIN's revenue in 2H26e, more than double 2025.** The competitive detail: **a CW laser project with "a major US optical chip IDM," mass production at YE27** — the first dated timeline on this page for third-party capacity entering the ultra-high-power/CW laser bottleneck that BTG's LightCounting expert (06-25) described as effectively a two-supplier moat with Coherent on one side. Not a 2026 threat; a 2028 one. Tape: **optical derated -15% since the June 22 SOX peak** (Jefferies). Cross-filed [[LITE]], [[AAOI]], [[AXTI]], [themes/optical-cpo](themes/optical-cpo.md). |

Two things it adds: (i) an independent, non-broker corroboration of Citi's 07-16 "InP Supply Constraint Will Likely Persist" model, with the sharper structural reason — **"80% of InP already going to AI" vs ~25% of DRAM, and InP has no consumer allocation to raid**, so there is no DRAM-style release valve (percentages directional per the author); (ii) the first appearance on this page of the **SOA-based ELSFP** alternative for NPO/CPO — better InP *area* efficiency but **17% gross margin vs 51%** for the conventional 8× discrete DFB build, with **COHR named among the firms that cannot make high-power SOA**. Net read for the page: the InP squeeze has an engineering workaround, it is uneconomic at current design points, and COHR is not in that lane — which is mildly *supportive* of the 6-inch-InP moat rather than a threat to it.
